CursorCorolla Point · 11%
Risk Draw 9 in a 22-runner York sprint is vulnerable if the low-draw speed horses get an easy lead.
confirmed early-pace map with three O'Meara runnersYork 5.5f draw split for 22 runners on Goodwhether Air Force One runs — CD 1-8 here with no listed price
Corolla Point is the form horse I trust most on true merit, not the steamed-up market leaders. He won Hamilton 5f on Good 18 days ago, was only 0.3L off at Newcastle over this trip at Class 2, and crucially finished third at York 5f on Good in May beaten just 0.2L — almost a course-and-distance blueprint for today. Edward Bethell’s yard is going well (T14 1-4), he handles top weight in this grade, and the 7.8→2.1 move reflects ability rather than a single soft win. Toca Madera’s draw 4 is ideal, but 0-9 on Good going and no win since 2023 make the 2.0 quote look like name recognition; I have him nearer 8% than implied 50%.

Lexington Blitz is the main danger: draw 1 at York in this field size is a material edge, Cowell is hot, and his Goodwood 5f Good win plus Epsom third show he belongs at Class 2 sprint level — the Goodwood sixth reads as a troubled run per stewards’ notes. The biggest threat to Corolla Point is tactical: if Stratusnine, Toca Madera or an O’Meara teammate burn hard from low draws, a mid-high stalker can find traffic or face a wall of horses. I still prefer Corolla Point’s proven York/Good profile over Blitz’s unproven course record (0-1 at York), but it is close.

Right The board pick Haffner @ 1.01 matched the result: the obvious market leader with Moore/O'Brien won at SP 1.0.

CursorAlfred Wallace · 24%
Risk Gentle Hurricane's market collapse-in-reverse and elite pedigree may hide superior ability on only his second start.
Haffner official ratingpace map and early speed profileswhether A P O'Brien prioritises Haffner over Confucius tactically
Alfred Wallace is the most bankable profile on today's terms: he has already won a Class 1 over seven furlongs on good ground at Ascot, carries the highest published OR (105), and arrives off a peak with two distance wins from three attempts. That Ascot form maps directly onto York — trip, going, and grade — whereas most rivals are stepping up in class, trip, or both. Ed Walker/Shoemark are in solid nick, and draw eight is workable in an eleven-runner field where York's seven-furlong bias is only mildly mid/high rather than punitive.

The biggest threat is not the market favourite on form alone but the uncertainty around Gentle Hurricane — one sharp six-furlong Salisbury win does not prove seven-furlong Class 1 stamina, yet Invincible Spirit/Pivotal, Balding, and Murphy with heavy support is a classic "better than the book" juvenile pattern. Haffner is the main danger on exposed credentials: his Goodwood second, beaten a neck in an identical 7f Good Class 1, is arguably the best piece of form in the race, and Ryan Moore's booking ahead of Confucius signals O'Brien's priority; wide draw eleven is a nagging negative, not a knockout.

Warner's Cross deserves respect after the Uttoxeter win at 20f on good ground, but he is 0-0 at Worcester and his profile is flatter trip-wise than Jewel's.

The biggest risk is completion, not ability: pulled up at Stratford and Huntingdon before the Worcester rebound, and at ten she is not a reliable jumper every time. If she skips a flight, Warner's Cross or the light-weight Gone In Sixty — already third over this C&D — are the most logical beneficiaries in a wide, competitive handicap where no runner deserves a short-priced “proof of winning” tag.

Thunder Home is the only runner with meaningful form at today's trip and grade: a neck-second at Sandown over 7f Class 3 on good ground shows he belongs at this level, and Billy Loughnane (card-leading jockey, strong recent strike rate) is a material plus. Draw 11 is neutral on Kempton's even 7f AW split; he has already raced this course, trip and surface at Class 3 (Lingfield AW, fifth of nine), so he is not walking in blind like most of the field. That Lingfield run caps enthusiasm, but it still beats backing a pure guess against ten unraced rivals.

The biggest danger is the debutant cluster from yards that routinely win these races — Gosden (Algaihabane, dr4), Balding (Perfect Heir), Owen/Fallon (Liberty's Hall, dr2), and Beckett (Shaddock) — any of whom could outclass Thunder Home on raw ability we cannot yet see. Sonic Angel's Newcastle AW win is eye-catching but came at six furlongs, off top weight 9-11, from draw 13; I would not treat that short-priced debut as proof he stays seven. Liberty's Hall is the main alternative: hot trainer-jockey combo and a low draw, but entirely unexposed. At roughly a one-in-seven chance, this is a marginal lean rather than a strong betting…

CursorRogue Defence · 19%
Risk Only three career runs — still unproven under a tight top-weight if the race develops strongly.
pace map and early speed distributionnon-runners affecting draw advantageexact AW ratings Lingfield-to-Kempton transfer
Rogue Defence is my marginal pick on true ability rather than price. Two Lingfield (AW) wins at this trip show he handles synthetic going and eight furlongs; draw six is fine in a nine-runner Kempton field where high draws only slightly help and should not override form. Billy Loughnane is the standout jockey on recent figures, Jack Jones gets a light 9-10 from OR 85, and the horse looks progressive rather than exposed. Cavolo Nero tops the weights on OR 87 but a 70-day gap and a cold Eustace/Fraser bench weaken the favourite case — I have him nearer 12% than the market’s ~29%. Malachy's Wish is the main danger: Lingfield AW winner eight days ago, OR 90 with room to improve as a three-year-old, though 10-1 and William Carver leave him short of Rogue Defence on jockey and weight terms.

The biggest risk to Rogue Defence is regression from a thin three-run profile in a 0-85 where Malachy's Wish and Talis Evolvere (draw eight, OR 80, close Chepstow run) can land a telling blow if he does not settle or travel. Helm Rock (CD 1-6) and Port Erin (strong distance record, Carroll yard in form) are each-live each-way angles at double-figure prices, not win picks.
